\mathrm{Sp}_{N}
is semisimple and simply connected implying that
H^{1}(F_{v},(G_{N})_{1\rtimes\theta})=0
by a standard result on
p
-adic groups.

7.2.1.
Local Result

We first study the indicator functions of cosets used to build the local factors of
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
:

Proposition 7.2.1
.

Let
N
be even, and choose
G=\mathrm{SO}_{N+1}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
(i.e,
\widehat{G}\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{N}
). Let
v
be a place of
F
, choose
k>0
, and let

\tilde{f}_{v,k}:=(J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\theta)\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{1,v}(k)}.

Then for all
\gamma\in Z_{G_{v}}
,
\tilde{f}_{v,k}^{G}(\gamma)=0
.

Proof.

First,
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
is the indicator function of the coset

K_{1,v}(k)(J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\theta)=K_{1,v}(k)\begin{pmatrix}&&&\pm\varpi_{v}^{-k}\\
&&\iddots&\\
&\pm\varpi_{v}^{-k}&&\\
1&&&\end{pmatrix}\rtimes\theta.

Elements of this coset have a non-zero entry in the bottom-left corner since elements of
K_{1,v}(k)
necessarily have a non-zero entry in the bottom-right when
k>0
.

However, the identity on
G
transfers to the stable class of
1\rtimes\theta
in
\widetilde{G}_{N}
and all elements in this class are of the form
A\rtimes\theta
with

w_{N}A^{T}w_{N}^{-1}=A.

In particular, since
N
is even, the signs in
w_{N}
force all elements in the stable class of
1\rtimes\theta
to have bottom left corner
a
satisfying
a=-a\implies a=0
. Therefore our coset doesn’t intersect the stable class that transfers to the identity so
\mathcal{O}_{1\rtimes\theta}(\tilde{f}_{v,k})=0
.

We then apply Proposition
7.1.6
. For the identity element,
\dim G_{1\rtimes\theta}=\dim H
so we only need to vanishing at
U=0
which follows by the above argument. Therefore
\mathcal{SO}_{1}(\tilde{f}_{v,k}^{H})=\tilde{f}_{v,k}^{H}(1)=0
. Since
\mathrm{SO}_{N+1}
has trivial center, we are done.
∎

7.2.2.
Global Result

We can then put these together to get a global result:

Corollary 7.2.2
.

Choose
G=\mathrm{SO}_{N+1}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
(i.e,
\widehat{G}\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{N}
). Choose conductor

\mathfrak{n}=\prod_{v}\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{v(\mathfrak{n})}.

If there exists finite place
v
satisfying either:

•

v(\mathfrak{n})
is odd,

•

v(\mathfrak{n})>N
,

then
(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(1)=0
. Otherwise,

(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(1)=\prod_{v}(-1)^{v(\mathfrak{n})/2}\binom{N/2}{v(\mathfrak{n})/2}.

Proof.

First,
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
has
\widetilde{E}_{v,v(\mathfrak{n})}
as its factor at
v
. By Proposition
4.3.2
,
\widetilde{E}_{v,v(\mathfrak{n})}
is a linear combination of the
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
from Proposition
7.2.1
with
\tilde{f}_{v,0}
appearing with coefficient
0
if the stated
conditions on
v
hold, and coefficient
(-1)^{v(\mathfrak{n})/2}\binom{N/2}{v(\mathfrak{n})/2}
otherwise.

Next, Proposition
7.2.1
gives that for
k>0
,
\tilde{f}_{v,k}^{H}(\gamma)=0
. The twisted fundamental lemma gives that
\tilde{f}_{v,0}^{H}(\gamma)=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{v}\rtimes\theta}^{H}(1)=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{H,v}}(1)=1
. The result follows.
∎

Note
.

When
N
is odd or
G\cong\mathrm{SO}_{N,\eta}
,
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
has positive trace against every cuspidal
A
-packet on
G
. Therefore, by Proposition
9.2.2
and Lemma
9.3.2
, we instead have that both

(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(1)\pm(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(-1)>0.

so the above computation needs to somehow fail to not contradict.

When
N
is odd, we can see this in the failure of the argument of Proposition
7.1.6
due to the negative sign for the bottom-left corner becoming positive. When
N
is even and
\widehat{G}
is orthogonal,
G
no longer has the same dimension as
(G_{N,v})_{\theta}
, so the formula of Corollary
7.1.8
doesn’t apply and non-trivial unipotent orbital integrals in
\widetilde{G}_{N,v}
contribute to central values of
(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}
.

8.
Application to
(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}
: Conjugate Self-Dual Case

The conjugate self-dual case is significantly more complicated. As preliminary computations:

8.1.
Twisted Conjugacy Classes

8.1.1.
Transfers

As in the self-dual case, the stable class of
1\rtimes\bar{\theta}\in\widetilde{G}_{N}
transfers to the identity in any
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
since
\bar{\theta}
fixes the standard pinning. Also,

(G_{N})_{1\rtimes\bar{\theta}}=U^{E/F}_{N}.

As in the generalization after
[
KS99
, 5.1.C]
, we can compute transfers from
Z_{G_{N}}(F_{v})\rtimes\bar{\theta}
to
Z_{G}(F_{v})
:

\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}\mapsto\beta/\bar{\beta},

implicitly using
Z_{G_{N}}(F_{v})=E_{v}^{\times}
and
Z_{G}(F_{v})=U_{1}(E_{v})
. This map is surjective onto
Z_{G}(F_{v})
by Hilbert 90.

8.1.2.
The Stable Class of the Identity

We compute how the stable “identity class”
1\rtimes\bar{\theta}
decomposes into rational classes. We recall (e.g.
[
Lab11
, §III.3]
) that for
\gamma
semisimple, the rational classes inside the stable class of
\gamma
are in bijection with

\ker(H^{1}(F,G^{0}_{\gamma})\to H^{1}(F,G)).

Applying this formula, consider the non-trivial case when
v
is non-split. Then

\ker(H^{1}(F_{v},U^{E_{w}/F_{v}}_{N})\to H^{1}(F_{v},\Res_{F_{v}}^{E_{w}}\mathrm{GL}_{N}))=H^{1}(F_{v},U^{E_{w}/F_{v}}_{N})=\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}

by Shapiro’s lemma, Hilbert 90, and Kottwitz’s formula from
[
Kot86
]
for the unitary cohomology group. Therefore, the stable conjugacy class of
1\rtimes\bar{\theta}
breaks up into two rational conjugacy classes.

We will need to distinguish the rational classes. Working over
\overline{F}_{v}
,
\widetilde{G}_{N}(\overline{F}_{v})\cong(\mathrm{GL}_{N}\times\mathrm{GL}_{N})(\overline{F}_{v})
and conjugation acts by swapping the coordinates, so the stable conjugacy class over
F_{v}
consists of all elements of the form
B\rtimes\bar{\theta}
with
B\in G_{N}(F_{v})\cong\mathrm{GL}_{N}(E_{w})
and

B=\bar{\theta}(B)^{-1}\;(=w_{N}\bar{B}^{T}w_{N}).

In particular,
\det B=\det\bar{B}
, so
B\rtimes\theta\mapsto\det(B)
is a map from the stable conjugacy class to
F_{v}^{\times}
. We can also compute that all values of this invariant can be realized by matrices in the stable class (e.g, pick an appropriate anti-diagonal matrix).

Since

A(B\rtimes\bar{\theta})A^{-1}=ABw_{N}\bar{A}^{T}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta},

rational conjugacy can only scale the above determinant invariant by elements of
N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}(E_{w}^{\times})
. In total,

B\rtimes\bar{\theta}\mapsto\det(B)\in F_{v}^{\times}/N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}(E_{w}^{\times})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}

is a complete invariant determining the rational conjugacy class of
B\rtimes\bar{\theta}
.

For
B\rtimes\bar{\theta}
in the stable class of arbitrary
\gamma\rtimes\bar{\theta}
for
\gamma\in Z_{G_{N,v}}
, we can instead use the invariant
\det(B)/\gamma^{N}\in F_{v}^{\times}/N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}(E_{w}^{\times})
.

8.1.3.
Transfer Factor Lemmas

We finally need some information about transfer factors for
G=U^{+}_{N}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
:

Lemma 8.1.1
.

Consider
G=U^{+}_{N}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
and
e\rtimes\bar{\theta}
a representative for the other rational class in the stable class of
1\rtimes\bar{\theta}
. Then for any choice of transfer factors:

\Delta^{\widetilde{G}_{N}}_{G}(1,1\rtimes\bar{\theta})=\Delta^{\widetilde{G}_{N}}_{G}(1,e\rtimes\bar{\theta}).

Proof.

Any strongly regular
\gamma\rtimes\bar{\theta}
close enough to
1\rtimes\bar{\theta}
would have stable conjugate
\gamma^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
close to
e\rtimes\bar{\theta}
. By Corollary
7.1.3
, it suffices to show the result for all such pairs
\gamma\rtimes\bar{\theta}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
in some small enough neighborhood.

For this, we apply
[
KS99
, 5.1.D]
. The hypercocycle
\kappa_{T}
is determined by the cocycle
a_{T}
defined on pp. 39-40 in loc. cit. Using notation therein, in the case of
H=U^{+}_{N}
, we have that
\widehat{H}=\widehat{G}_{1}
exactly. Therefore
a_{T}
is trivial so
\kappa_{T}
is as well. The result follows.
∎

Lemma 8.1.2
.

Consider
G=U^{+}_{N}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
, and semisimple
\gamma\rtimes\bar{\theta}\in\widetilde{G}_{N,v}
and
\delta\in G_{v}
. Then, for all
a\in E^{\times}=Z_{G_{N,v}}
,

\Delta^{\widetilde{G}_{N}}_{G}(\delta,\gamma)=\Delta^{\widetilde{G}_{N}}_{G}((a/\bar{a})\delta,a\gamma).

Proof.

This follows from the generalization after
[
KS99
, 5.1.C]
. The character
\lambda_{C}
there must be trivial to be consistent with Lemma
5.2.4
, see
[
Mok15
, Rmk 3.2.3]
.

Note that for the non-strongly regular case, we prove this first for strongly regular classes close to
\gamma
and
\delta
and then use Corollary/Definition
7.1.3
.
∎

8.2.
Number-Theoretic Lemmas

We also need two number-theoretic lemmas. Beware an indexing technicality: at places
v
of
F
that ramify in
E
to places
w
, we state all results in terms of powers of
\mathfrak{p}_{w}
. Let
\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
be the different ideal.

Lemma 8.2.1
.

Define the map

\phi:\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times},\quad\phi(x)=\frac{x}{\bar{x}}.

Then

\phi(1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k})\subseteq 1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}\cap\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}

Furthermore, if
E_{w}/F_{v}
is at most tamely ramified or
k=0
, then

\phi(1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k})=(1+(\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}\cap\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}))_{Nm=1}.

Proof.

For all
x\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}}
, we have
\phi(x)\equiv 1\mod\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
since
x-\bar{x}\in\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
by
[
Ser13
, IV.2]
. This gives the inclusion.

For the equality, let
j\geq 0
be such that
\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}=\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{j}
. It suffices to show that
H^{1}(\Gal(E_{w}/F_{v}),1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k})
vanishes for all
k\geq j
. If
E_{w}
is unramified, then
\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}=\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}
, and the vanishing for all
k
follows from
[
NSW13
, VII.7.1.2]
. This lemma also gives the desired vanishing tamely ramified case for
k\geq 1
(since then
\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}=\mathfrak{p}_{w}
).

It remains to check the case when
v
is wildly ramified and
k=0
: that
\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times})=(1+(\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}\cap\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}))_{Nm=1}
. This is equivalent to checking that in the long exact sequence associated to

0\to 1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}}/(1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}})\to 0,

the natural map
H^{1}(\Gal(E_{w}/F_{v}),1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}})\to H^{1}(\Gal(E_{w}/F_{v}),\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}})
is trivial. We check instead that

(8.2.1)

H^{1}(\Gal(E_{w}/F_{v}),\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}})\hookrightarrow H^{1}(\Gal(E_{w}/F_{v}),\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}}/(1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}})).

A computation with the long exact sequence associated to
\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}\hookrightarrow E_{w}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}
shows that
H^{1}(\Gal(E_{w}/F_{v}),\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
, generated by the class of
\varpi/\overline{\varpi}
. To get (
8.2.1
), it suffices to show that this class survives under reduction modulo
1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}=:1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{j}
. Since
\varpi/\overline{\varpi}\in(1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{j-1})\setminus(1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{j})

[
Ser13
, IV.1-2]
, the cocycle is nontrivial in the quotient. Furthermore, since
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{w}}/(1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}})
is a trivial Galois module, any coboundary is trivial. In total,
\varpi/\overline{\varpi}
represents a nontrivial class.
∎

Lemma 8.2.2
.

Let
N
be odd. There is
A\in\mathrm{GL}_{N}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}})
such that
w_{N}\bar{A}^{T}w_{N}^{-1}=yA
if and only if
N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}(y)=1
and either:

(1)

E_{w}/F_{v}
is unramified,

(2)

E_{w}/F_{v}
is tamely ramified and
y^{N}\not\equiv-1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
,

(3)

E_{w}/F_{v}
is wildly ramified and
y^{N}\notin 1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}
or
y\in 1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
.

Proof.

We instead construct
B=w_{N}A\in\mathrm{GL}_{N}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}})
, which would have to satisfy:

\bar{B}^{T}=\bar{A}^{T}w_{N}^{-1}=w_{N}^{-1}w_{N}\bar{A}^{T}w_{N}^{-1}=(-1)^{N-1}w_{N}yA=yB.

If
y
can be written as
\alpha/\overline{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
, then
B
can be chosen to be diagonal with
\alpha
’s on the diagonal.

If not, then any such
B
has only zeros on the diagonal. Let its entries be
b_{ij}
so that

\det B=\sum_{\sigma}\sgn(\sigma)\prod_{i}b_{i\sigma(i)},

where by the assumption on
B
, we can sum over just permutations that don’t have fixed points. Comparing terms for
\sigma
and
\sigma^{-1}
,

\sgn(\sigma^{-1})\prod_{i}b_{i\sigma^{-1}(i)}=\sgn(\sigma)\prod_{i}b_{\sigma(i)i}=\sgn(\sigma)y^{N}\prod_{i}\bar{b}_{i\sigma(i)}.

Since
N
is odd, all permutations with no fixed points aren’t equal to their inverses so we can therefore pair them up and write
\det B
in the form
R+y^{N}\bar{R}
where
R
can be freely chosen to be any element of
\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}
.

If
y^{N}\not\equiv-1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{v}}
, this can be arranged to be in
\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}
by choosing
R\in\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}^{\times}
. Otherwise, there is
R
such that
R+y^{N}\bar{R}\in\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}
if and only if there is
R
such that
R/\bar{R}\not\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{v}}
. Summarizing, the possible
y
’s are exactly those of norm
1
for which at least one of the following hold:

(1)

There is
R\in\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}
such that
R/\bar{R}\not\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
,

(2)

y
can be written in the form
\alpha/\overline{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}
,

(3)

y^{N}\not\equiv-1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
.

As in Lemma
8.2.1
, (1) holds if and only if
E_{w}/F_{v}
is unramified. For
E_{w}/F_{v}
tamely ramified, (2) implies that
y\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
which together with
\mathfrak{p}_{v}\nmid 2
implies (3).

If
E_{w}/F_{v}
is wildly ramified, then
\mathfrak{p}_{w}|2
so (3) is equivalent to
y^{N}\notin 1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}
. Also (2) in our setting is equivalent to
y\in 1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
by Lemma
8.2.1
.
∎

8.3.
Local Results

Now we are ready for the main results. We again study coset indicators
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
used to build up
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
in Corollary
4.3.1
. Beware that we use local indexing for
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
; see §
3.1.2
.

8.3.1.
Non-Intersection of Support

Proposition 8.3.1
.

In the conjugate self-dual case, let
N
be even and
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
. Let
v
be a place of
F
that doesn’t split in
E
and
w
the corresponding place of
E
. Let

\tilde{f}_{v,k}:=(J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{1,v}(k)}.

Then for all
\gamma\in Z_{G_{v}}\subseteq E_{w}^{\times}
, the support of
\tilde{f}_{v,k}^{G}
intersects the stable conjugacy class of
\gamma
only if:

(1)

-\gamma\in\phi(1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k})
(see Lemma
8.2.1
), and:

(2)

either:

(a)

E_{w}/F_{v}
is unramified,

(b)

E_{w}/F_{v}
is ramified and
k
is even.

Proof.

As explained in
8.1.1
, the element
\gamma\in U_{E/F}(1)_{v}=Z_{G_{v}}
transfers to the stable conjugacy class
\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}
of
\widetilde{G}_{N}
with
\beta/\bar{\beta}=\gamma
. This stable class consists exactly of elements of the form

(8.3.1)

\beta A\rtimes\bar{\theta}\text{ with }w_{N}\overline{A}^{T}w_{N}^{-1}=A.

We want to compare this stable class with the support of
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
, which
consists of all elements of the form

X(J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})=X\begin{pmatrix}&&&\pm\varpi_{v}^{-k}\\
&&\iddots&\\
&\pm\varpi_{v}^{-k}&&\\
1&&&\end{pmatrix}\rtimes\bar{\theta}

for
X\in K_{1,v}(k)
. We write
X
in block matrix form

(8.3.2)

X=\begin{pmatrix}X_{0}&\vec{v}_{1}\\
\vec{v}_{2}&x\end{pmatrix}\iff X(J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})=\begin{pmatrix}\vec{v}_{1}&\varpi_{v}^{-k}X_{0}w_{N-1}\\
x&\varpi_{v}^{-k}\vec{v}_{2}w_{N-1}\end{pmatrix}\rtimes\bar{\theta}.

We now assume that there exists such an element
X(J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})
in the stable class of
\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}
and derive restrictions on
\gamma
.

Necessary Condition 1:

As in the proof of Proposition
7.2.1
, we have a restriction from the bottom-left matrix entry
x
of
X(J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\theta)
. Since
N
is even, condition (
8.3.1
) gives that
x=\beta\alpha
for
\alpha
satisfying
\overline{\alpha}=-\alpha
. However, we also have
x\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}}
. Therefore, the class of
\beta\rtimes\theta
only intersects the support of
f_{k}
if there is
\alpha\in E_{w}
such that:

(8.3.3)

\displaystyle\bar{\alpha}=-\alpha,\quad\alpha\beta\in\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}},\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha\beta\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}}.

This is equivalent to something simpler: if
\alpha,\beta
satisfy conditions (
8.3.3
), then there is
h=\alpha\beta-1\in\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}
such that

\gamma=-(1+h)/(1+\bar{h}).

Conversely, given
h\in\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}
,
\gamma
satisfying the above equation, and
\beta
such that
\beta/\bar{\beta}=\gamma
, setting
\alpha=(1+h)/\beta
satisfies conditions (
8.3.3
). Finally, by Lemma
8.2.1
, the
\gamma=-(1+h)/(1+\bar{h})
condition is further equivalent to

(8.3.4)

-\gamma\in\phi(1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k})

for the map
\phi
of Lemma
8.2.1
. This is condition (1).

Necessary Condition 2:

We also have a restriction from the top-right
(N-1)\times(N-1)
block in the right-hand side of (
8.3.2
). Condition (
8.3.1
) requires that

w_{N-1}\overline{(\beta^{-1}\varpi^{-k}X_{0}w_{N-1})}^{T}w_{N-1}^{-1}=-(\beta^{-1}\varpi^{-k}X_{0}w_{N-1}),

where the minus sign appears since “restricting” the conjugation by
w_{N}
to the block doesn’t exactly give
w_{N-1}
. In particular
X_{0}w_{N-1}
is an eigenvector for the conjugate-linear involution on
GL_{N_{1}}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}})
given by
Y\mapsto w_{N-1}\overline{Y}^{T}w_{N-1}^{-1}
, with eigenvalue

-(\overline{\beta}/\beta)(\overline{\varpi}^{k}/\varpi^{k})=-\gamma^{-1}(\overline{\varpi}^{k}/\varpi^{k}).

Assume that
k>0
. Since
\vec{v}_{2}\equiv 0\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}}
and
X
is invertible over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}
, the matrix
X_{0}
, and therefore
X_{0}w_{N-1}
must also be invertible over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}
.

In particular,
-\gamma^{-1}(\overline{\varpi}^{k}/\varpi^{k})
satisfies the conditions of Lemma
8.2.2
.

Sufficiency

In the converse direction, if
\gamma
and
\beta
satisfy (
8.3.3
) and
y=-\gamma^{-1}(\overline{\varpi}/\varpi)
satisfies the conditions of Lemma
8.2.2
for
\mathrm{GL}_{N-1}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}})
, we can choose
X_{0}w_{N-1}\in\mathrm{GL}_{N-1}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}})
satisfying

w_{N-1}\overline{(X_{0}w_{N-1})^{T}}w_{N-1}^{-1}=-(\overline{\beta}/\beta)(\overline{\varpi}^{k}/\varpi^{k})(X_{0}w_{N-1}).

Then choosing
\alpha
as above, the matrix

A=\begin{pmatrix}&\varpi^{-k}\beta^{-1}X_{0}w_{N-1}\\
\alpha&\end{pmatrix}

satisfies
w_{N}\bar{A}^{T}w_{N}^{-1}=A
and
\beta A\rtimes\bar{\theta}
is in the support of
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
.

Simplification of Condition 2

Finally, (
8.3.4
) lets us simplify condition 2. By Lemma
8.2.1
, condition (
8.3.4
) gives that
-\gamma^{-1}(\varpi/\overline{\varpi})^{-k}\equiv(\varpi/\overline{\varpi})^{-k}
mod both
\mathfrak{p}_{w}
and
\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
. This congruence class mod either ideal is independent of the choice of
\varpi
since different choices make the quotient vary only by
\alpha/\bar{\alpha}\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}}
for
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}
. Our desired conditions from Lemma
8.2.2
therefore become

•

If
v
is unramified: nothing

•

If
v
is tamely ramified:
(\varpi/\overline{\varpi})^{-(N-1)k}\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}

•

If
v
is wildly ramified:
(\varpi/\overline{\varpi})^{-(N-1)k}\not\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
or
(\varpi/\overline{\varpi})^{-k}\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}}
.

A tamely ramified quadratic extension is generated by a square root of a uniformizer, so in this case
\varpi/\overline{\varpi}\equiv-1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
. Therefore, since
N-1
is odd, the condition in Lemma
8.2.2
becomes that
k
is even.

In the wildly ramified case, we note since
\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
is exactly generated by
\varpi-\overline{\varpi}
, we have that
\varpi/\overline{\varpi}
is a non-trivial element of
(1+\varpi^{-1}\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}})/(1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}})
. In particular
(\varpi/\overline{\varpi})\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}}
always. On the other hand, since
v|2
, the quotient is a
2
-group so we again have that
(\varpi/\overline{\varpi})^{-k}\in 1+\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
holds if and only if
k
is even.
∎

8.3.2.
Orbital Integral Vanishing

Our support result immediately gives:

Corollary 8.3.2
.

Consider the setup of Proposition
8.3.1
. For all
\gamma\in Z_{G_{v}}
that do not satisfy the conditions therein, we have
f^{G}_{v,k}(\gamma)=0
.

Proof.

Since
\dim(G_{v})=\dim((G_{N,v})_{\gamma\rtimes\theta})
, this follows from Proposition
7.1.6
.
∎

In the case of
G=U^{+}_{N}
, we can say more:

Proposition 8.3.3
.

Consider the setup of Proposition
8.3.1
and specialize to the case where
G=U_{N}^{+}
. Assume that
\gamma
satisfies the conditions therein. Then:

(1)

if
\gamma^{\prime}
also satisfies the conditions of Proposition
8.3.1
, then
\tilde{f}^{G}_{v,k}(\gamma)=\tilde{f}^{G}_{v,k}(\gamma^{\prime})
,

(2)

if
v
is unramified,
f_{v,k}^{G}(\gamma)
is non-zero with sign
(-1)^{k}
,

(3)

if
v
is ramified,
f_{v,k}^{G}(\gamma)=0
whenever
k>0
.

Proof.

We prove the points in order:

Constancy

For
i=1,2
, consider
\gamma_{i}\in Z_{G_{v}}
satisfying the conditions of Proposition
8.3.1
and let
\gamma_{i}
be a norm of
\beta_{i}\rtimes\bar{\theta}\in Z_{G_{N,v}}\rtimes\theta
; we can choose
\beta_{i}\equiv a\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}}
for some
a\in E^{\times}_{w}
such that
\bar{a}=-a
. Let
z=\beta_{2}/\beta_{1}
, noting that
z\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}}
so that
z\in K_{1,v}(k)
. Let
e=z/\bar{z}=\gamma_{2}/\gamma_{1}
.

First, by Lemma
8.1.2
,

\Delta^{\widetilde{G}}_{G}(e\gamma,z\widetilde{\gamma})=\Delta^{\widetilde{G}}_{G}(\gamma,\widetilde{\gamma}).

Second,
z\in K_{1,v}(k)
gives that
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
is invariant under translation by
z
so
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\tilde{f}_{v,k})=\mathcal{O}_{z\widetilde{\gamma}}(\tilde{f}_{v,k})
for any
\widetilde{\gamma}\in\widetilde{G}_{N,v}
. Let
\beta_{i}^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
be a representative for the other rational class in the stable class of
\beta_{i}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
. Putting everything together, the formula in Corollary
7.1.8
shows that

(8.3.5)

\tilde{f}_{v,k}^{G}(\gamma_{1})\\
=e(G_{\beta_{1}\rtimes\bar{\theta}})\Delta^{\widetilde{G}}_{G}(\gamma_{1},\beta_{1}\rtimes\bar{\theta})\mathcal{O}_{\beta_{1}\rtimes\bar{\theta}}(\tilde{f}_{v,k})+e(G_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}})\Delta^{\widetilde{G}}_{G}(\gamma_{1},\beta_{1}^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta})\mathcal{O}_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}}(\tilde{f}_{v,k})\\
=e(G_{\beta_{2}\rtimes\bar{\theta}})\Delta^{\widetilde{G}}_{G}(\gamma_{2},\beta_{2}\rtimes\bar{\theta})\mathcal{O}_{\beta_{2}\rtimes\bar{\theta}}(\tilde{f}_{v,k})+e(G_{\beta_{2}^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}})\Delta^{\widetilde{G}}_{G}(\gamma_{2},\beta_{2}^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta})\mathcal{O}_{\beta_{2}^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}}(\tilde{f}_{v,k})\\
=\tilde{f}_{v,k}^{G}(\gamma_{2}).

This proves the equality we want.

Unramified Calculation

For the unramified calculation, choose again
\gamma\in Z_{G_{v}}
that is a norm of
\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}\in Z_{G_{N,v}}\rtimes\theta
such that the other rational class inside the stable class of
\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}
is represented by
\beta^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
.

In the unramified case,
N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}(E_{w}^{\times})
consists of all elements of even valuation. Therefore, by the discussion in §
8.1.2
, we can without loss of generality choose the conjugation orbit of
\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}
to be only supported on elements of even parity while that of
\beta^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
is only on those with odd parity. Note that since
\det\beta
has even parity, this also implies that
G_{\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}}
is the quasisplit inner form of
G_{v}
while
G_{\beta^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}}
is the non-quasisplit one. In particular, since
N
is even,
e(G_{\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}})=1
and
e(G_{\beta^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}})=-1
.

However, on the support of
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
, the determinant has constant valuation
-k(N-1)
which has the same parity as
k
. Therefore, only one of the orbital integrals in (
8.3.5
) is non-zero—the one for
\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}
if
k
is even and that for
\beta^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
if
k
is odd.

Finally, Lemma
8.1.1
forces the two transfer factors to have the same sign. The consistency of our choice with the twisted fundamental lemma forces this common sign to be positive. Combining this with the above two paragraphs finishes the sign computation.

Ramified Calculation

As in the unramified calculation, choose
\gamma,\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta},\beta^{\prime}\rtimes\bar{\theta}
. This computation is more convenient to do using a different presentation of
\widetilde{G}_{N,v}
. We realize

\widetilde{G}_{N}(E_{w})\cong\mathrm{GL}_{N}(E_{w})^{2}\rtimes s

where
s
is the involution switching the coordinates and
\widetilde{G}_{N,v}
is embedded through

g(J^{-1}_{\mathfrak{n}}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})\mapsto(g,J^{-1}_{\mathfrak{n}}\bar{g}^{-T}J_{\mathfrak{n}})\rtimes s.

If
E_{w}/F_{v}
is ramified there is
\epsilon\in\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}\setminus N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}(E_{w}^{\times})
. Consider the
N\times N
matrix

E=\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon&&&\\
&\ddots&&\\
&&\epsilon&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}.

Recall the form of elements in
K_{1,v}(k)(J_{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})
given in (
8.3.2
). If
k\geq 1
, any such element additionally of the form in (
8.3.1
) must satisfy
\vec{v}_{1}=\vec{v}_{2}=0
since otherwise their coordinates have different valuations but must also be equal up to sign. In particular,
E
commutes with all
X
such that
X(J_{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})
is of the form of both (
8.3.2
) and (
8.3.1
). Furthermore,
J^{-1}_{\mathfrak{n}}\bar{E}^{-T}J_{\mathfrak{n}}=E
, so in total,

(E,1)X(J_{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta})(E,1)^{-1}=EX(J_{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\bar{\theta}).

considering
(E,1)
as an element of
G_{N}(E_{w})=\mathrm{GL}_{N}(E_{w})\times\mathrm{GL}_{N}(E_{w})
. In particular, this preserves both the conditions of (
8.3.2
) and (
8.3.1
)—in other words, conjugation by
(E,1)
sends the intersection of support of
\tilde{f}_{v,k}
with the stable orbit of any
\beta\rtimes\bar{\theta}
for
\beta\in Z_{G,v}
to itself.

Finally, note that since
N
is even, the determinants of
XE
and
X
descend to the two different classes in
F_{v}^{\times}/N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}(E_{w}^{\times})
so conjugation by
(E,1)
also swaps the two rational orbits in the stable orbit. Since the measures on the different centralizers used to define orbital integrals in a stable class are related by conjugation in the algebraic closure, this gives that the two orbital integrals in (
8.3.5
) are equal. By the same argument as the unramified calculation, this implies that the transfer must vanish.
∎

We need two extra arguments. When
v
is ramified and
k=0
we will use the spectral arguments of Section
9
:

Lemma 8.3.4
.

Consider the same setup as Proposition
8.3.1
and assume additionally that
G=U^{+}_{N}
,
v
is ramified, and
k=0
. Then for
\gamma\in Z_{G_{v}}
,
\tilde{f}_{v,0}^{G}(\gamma)>0
whenever
\gamma\equiv-1\pmod{\mathfrak{D}_{E_{w}/F_{v}}}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.3.3
(1), it suffices to show that
\tilde{f}_{v,0}^{G}(-1)>0
. However, as in Theorem
4.3.6
, the function
\delta_{a}\star\tilde{f}_{v,0}
is trace-positive since unramified representations have trivial standard root number. Therefore, since conjugate self-dual unramified representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{N}(E_{w})
exist and are both conjugate-orthogonal and -symplectic, Proposition
9.2.2
shows that
(\delta_{a}\star\tilde{f}_{v,0})^{G}(1)>0
.

Finally, by Lemma
8.1.2
,
(\delta_{a}\star\tilde{f}_{v,0})^{G}(1)=\tilde{f}_{v,0}^{G}(a/\bar{a})=\tilde{f}_{v,0}^{G}(-1)
.
∎

Finally, in the case of
v
split:

Lemma 8.3.5
.

Consider the same setup as Proposition
8.3.1
except that
v
now splits in
E
and we allow odd
N
. Then we can choose

(\tilde{f}_{v,k})^{G}=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{1,v}(k)}.

In particular

\tilde{f}_{v,1}^{G}(\gamma)=\begin{cases}\vol(K_{1,v}(k))^{-1}&\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}\text{ and }\gamma\equiv 1\pmod{\mathfrak{p}^{k}_{v}}\\
0&\text{ else}\end{cases}.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma 6.1.1(3) in
[
DGG22
]
using that
J_{v,k}^{-1}w_{N}\rtimes\theta
is an involution that normalizes
K_{1,v}(k)
.
∎

8.4.
Global Result

We now put these together to get a global vanishing result:

Corollary 8.4.1
.

Let
N
be even and consider
G=U_{N,+}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
. Fix a conductor

\mathfrak{n}=\prod_{v}\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k_{v}}

globally indexed as in §
3.1.2
. If there exists a ramified place
v
of
F
such that either:

•

k_{v}
is half-integral,

•

k_{v}>N/2
,

then
(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(\gamma)=0
for all
\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)
.

Proof.

Recall the choice of
a\in E^{\times}
such that
a/\bar{a}=-1
from Definition
4.3.4
. Let
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}}
be as in Proposition
8.3.1
except globally indexed. Then
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is a linear combination of terms of the form

\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}=\prod_{v\text{ split}}\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k_{v}}}\prod_{v\text{ ns}}\delta_{a^{N-1}}\star\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k_{v}}}.

First, note that by Lemma
8.1.2
, for all test functions
\widetilde{\varphi}
on
\widetilde{G}_{N,v}
,

(\delta_{a^{N-1}}\star\widetilde{\varphi})^{G}(\gamma)=\widetilde{\varphi}^{G}((-1)^{N-1}\gamma).

Therefore, by Proposition
8.3.3
, we have
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}^{G}(\gamma)=0
for all
\gamma\in Z_{G,v}
if there is a ramified
v
such that
v(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime})>0
. By Proposition
4.3.2
, the bulleted conditions exactly guarantee that all of the
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
making up
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
satisfy this.
∎

Unlike in the self-dual case of Corollary
7.2.2
, without the conditions on
\mathfrak{n}
, there can be multiple summands
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
with non-zero transfers appearing with different signs. We of course expect the terms (
8.4.1
) for these
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
to have very different magnitudes and therefore not cancel. However, the techniques established here do not allow us to computing fine enough information on the magnitudes of transfers to show this. We therefore give a partial non-vanishing result which will allow us, in some instances, to describe the bias in signs as the weight varies:

Corollary 8.4.2
.

Consider the setup of Corollary
8.4.1
and assume that
\mathfrak{n}
does not satisfy the conditions therein. Let
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{ur}}
be the part of
\mathfrak{n}
without any ramified factors. Then

\prod_{v\nmid\infty}(-1)^{k_{v}}\sum_{\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)}\omega(\gamma)(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(\gamma)>0

for any character
\omega
on
Z_{G}\subseteq E^{\times}
such that
\omega
is trivial on
(1+\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{ur}}\mathfrak{D}_{E/F})\cap\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
and non-trivial on
(1+\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\mathfrak{D}_{E/F})\cap\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
for all proper
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{ur}}
.

Proof.

Recall the notation in the proof of Corollary
8.4.1
, in particular the choice of
a\in E^{\times}
such that
\bar{a}/a=-1
. As in that proof, by Lemma
8.1.2
, all test functions
\widetilde{\varphi}
on
\widetilde{G}_{N,v}
satisfy

(\delta_{a^{N-1}}\star\widetilde{\varphi})^{G}(\gamma)=\widetilde{\varphi}^{G}((-1)^{N-1}\gamma).

The function
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is a linear combination of
f_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
for conductors
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{n}
. If
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
has a ramified divisor, Proposition
8.3.3
(3) and Corollary
8.3.2
give that
f^{G}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma)=0
for all
\gamma
.

If
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\mid\mathfrak{n}
has no ramified divisors, multiply together the appropriate cases of Corollary
8.3.2
, Proposition
8.3.3
(1), and Lemmas
8.3.5
and
8.3.4
over all
v
. This gives that for
\gamma\in Z_{G}
,
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}(\gamma)^{G}
is non-zero and constant for
\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
with
\gamma\equiv-1\pmod{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\mathfrak{D}_{E/F}}
and zero otherwise (using the
k=0
case of Lemma
8.2.1
to find the image of
\phi
). Therefore, for such
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}

(8.4.1)

\sum_{\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)}\omega(\gamma)\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}^{G}(\gamma)\neq 0

whenever
\omega
is trivial on
(1+\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\mathfrak{D}_{E/F})\cap\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
and is
0
otherwise. Furthermore, when this term is non-zero, Proposition
8.3.3
(2) shows that it has sign
\prod_{v\text{ ur}}(-1)^{k_{v}}
.

If
\mathfrak{n}
does not satisfy the conditions of Corollary
8.4.1
, then Proposition
4.3.2
says that there is
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
appearing in the linear combination defining
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
with
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
having no ramified divisors. Note that all such
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
divide
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{ur}}
. The conditions on
\omega
guarantee that (
8.4.1
) vanishes for all of these
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
except
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{ur}}
itself. Therefore the result follows after noting that
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{ur}}}
appears with coefficient
\prod_{v\text{ ram.}}(-1)^{k_{v}}
in
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
.
∎

Of course, we expect the full result:

Conjecture 8.4.3
.

Corollary
8.4.2
should hold for all
\omega
that are trivial on
(1+\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{ur}}\mathfrak{D}_{E/F})\cap\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
.

9.
A Spectral Argument and
(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}

We will also need to understand the main term (
6.2.1
) for
(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}
. Since
C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\infty}
was defined spectrally, this will necessarily happen through a spectral argument:

9.1.
A Stable Fourier Inversion Formula

9.1.1.
Setup

Specifically, we use a “stable” Fourier inversion result for a formally defined “stable” Fourier transform. Fix
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in either the self-dual or conjugate self-dual case and a finite set of finite places
S
. Let
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{temp}}
be the tempered unitary dual of
G_{S}
.

Let
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}
be the set of tempered
L
-packets/parameters for
G_{S}
(or in the case of
\mathrm{SO}_{2n}
, outer-automorphism orbits of these). Since tempered
L
-packets partition tempered representations by either
[
Art13
, Thm 1.5.1(b)]
or
[
Mok15
, Thm 2.5.1(b)]
, there is a map

\rho:\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{temp}}\to\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}.

Associated to
\rho
, we have pushforward and normalized pullback maps between functions on
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{temp}}
and those on
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}
:

\rho_{*}f(\Pi)=\sum_{\pi\in\rho^{-1}(\Pi)}f(\pi),\qquad\bar{\rho}^{*}f(\pi)=|\rho^{-1}(\rho(\pi))|^{-1}f\circ\rho(\pi),

where
|\rho^{-1}(\rho(\pi))|
is the cardinality of the packet
\Pi
containing
\pi
.
Dual to these we also have pullback and normalized pushforward between functionals on both spaces, denoted
\rho^{*},\bar{\rho}_{*}
. Note that for all functions
f
and functionals
\mu
,

\rho_{*}\bar{\rho}^{*}f=f,\qquad\bar{\rho}_{*}\rho^{*}\mu=\mu.

Conversely,
\bar{\rho}^{*}\rho_{*}f
and
\rho^{*}\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu
can be though of as averaging over
L
-packets.

Next, for any
U_{S}\subseteq G_{S}
such that
U_{S}\cap Z_{G_{S}}
is compact, define

\mathcal{PW}(G_{S},U_{S})=\{\widehat{f}:\check{G}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{S}\to\mathbb{C}:f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(U_{S})\},

the Paley-Wiener space of Fourier transforms of smooth, compactly supported functions with support in
U_{S}
. We can similarly define an unrestricted
\mathcal{PW}(G_{S})
of all compactly supported functions. By looking at the image under pushforward, we also define
\mathcal{PW}^{\mathrm{st}}(G_{S},U_{S})
and
\mathcal{PW}^{\mathrm{st}}(G_{S})
.

To avoid the use of the Ramanujan conjecture
3
3

3

this proven for regular integral infinitesimal character at infinity in many of our cases in
[
Shi11
]
,
[
KS20
]
,
[
KS23
]
through constructing associated Galois representations. However, these results are not yet comprehensive so we will instead use a lower-tech argument.
, we need to extend these constructions to the non-tempered specturm in way that is consistent with the endoscopic classification. Since non-tempered
L
-packets are not necessarily made up of irreducible representations, we need to work with a modified Fourier transform for non-tempered
\pi
:

\widehat{f}(\pi):=\tr_{\mathcal{I}_{\pi}}f

where
\mathcal{I}_{\pi}
is the unique parabolic induction of a tempered representation tensor a character that has
\pi
as a Langlands quotient. Then, using the definition of non-tempered
L
-packets (see e.g.
[
Art13
, pp. 44-45]
), there is an extension our map

\rho:\check{G}_{S}\to\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}

to have image in the space of all
L
-parameters such that for
f\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(G_{S})

\rho_{*}\widehat{f}(\Pi)=\tr_{\Pi}f

for even non-tempered
L
-packets
\Pi
. Of course, our modified
\widehat{f}
still satisfies the Fourier inversion formula since it is unchanged on the support of
\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}
.

9.1.2.
Global Input

Now we are ready for the main proof:

Proposition 9.1.1
.

Let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in either the self-dual (
G\neq\mathrm{SO}_{2})
or conjugate self-dual case. Let
S
be a finite set of finite places of
F
and
U_{S}\subseteq G_{S}
such that
U_{S}\cap Z_{G_{s}}
is compact. Then there exists a sequence of measures
\mu_{n}
on
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}
such that for all
\widehat{f}_{S}\in\mathcal{PW}(G_{S},U_{S})
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\rho^{*}\mu_{n}(\widehat{f}_{S})=\mu_{G_{S}}^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}).

Proof.

Since
Z_{G_{\infty}}
is compact for the groups
G
under consideration, there always exists compact open
K^{S,\infty}\subseteq G^{S,\infty}
such that
U_{S}\times K^{S,\infty}\cap Z_{G}(F)=1
. Let
\widehat{f}_{S}
be the Fourier transform of
f_{S}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(U_{S})
.

Let
\lambda
be a regular, integral infinitesimal character, and let

f_{\lambda}=(\dim\lambda)^{-1}\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f_{S}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{S,\infty}}.

Our strategy is to define

\mu_{\lambda}(\widehat{f}_{S}):=S_{\Sigma_{\eta}}^{G}(f_{\lambda}),

show that
\mu_{\lambda}
is indeed a measure on
\check{G}_{S}
pulled back from
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}
, and to show convergence to the Plancherel measure as
m(\lambda)\to\infty
.

First, the equalities
[
DGG22
, (5.3.1)]
together with the twisted endoscopic character identities for simple
A
-parameters shows that

\mu_{\lambda}(f_{\lambda})=S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(f_{\lambda})\\
=(\dim\lambda)^{-1}\sum_{\Pi\in\Sigma_{\eta,\lambda}}\left(\sum_{\pi^{S,\infty}\in\Pi^{S,\infty}}\dim((\pi^{S,\infty})^{K^{S,\infty}})\right)\left(\sum_{\pi_{S}\in\Pi_{S}}\tr_{\pi_{S}}(f_{S})\right),

which by inspection is of the form
\rho^{*}\mu_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{st}}
for some
\mu_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{st}}
on
\check{G}^{\mathrm{st}}_{S}
.

For the convergence, we have from Theorem
6.4.1
that

S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(f_{\lambda})=\tau^{\prime}(G)f_{S}(1)\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{S,\infty}}(1)+O(m(\lambda)^{-C})

using that our choice of
K^{S,\infty}
guarantees that all non-identity terms in the
sum (
6.2.1
) defining
\Lambda
vanish. Evaluating constants and using Fourier inversion shows that

\lim_{m(\lambda)\to\infty}S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\tau^{\prime}(G)^{-1}\vol(K^{S,\infty})f_{\lambda})=\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}).

Combining gives that

(9.1.1)

\lim_{m(\lambda)\to\infty}\rho^{*}\mu^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda}(\widehat{f}_{S})=\tau^{\prime}(G)\vol(K^{S,\infty})^{-1}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}).

which is what we wanted after choosing an appropriate sequence of
\lambda
.
∎

9.1.3.
Final Result

Rephrasing this in familiar language:

Theorem 9.1.2

(Stability of Plancherel Measure)
.

Let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in either the self-dual or conjugate self-dual case and
S
a finite set of finite places of
F
. Then for all
f_{S}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(G_{S})
,

f_{S}(1)=\int_{\check{G}_{S}}\tr_{\pi_{S}}(f_{S})\,d\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}(\pi_{S})=\int_{\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}}\sum_{\pi_{S}\in\Pi_{S}}\tr_{\pi_{S}}(f_{S})\,d\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}(\Pi_{S}).

Proof.

The first equality is by Fourier inversion. The second is equivalent to proving that for all
U_{S}
such that
U_{S}\cap Z_{G_{S}}
is compact and
\widehat{f}_{S}\in\mathcal{PW}(G_{S},U_{S})
,

\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S})=\rho^{*}\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}).

Let
\mathcal{PW}^{\mathrm{st}}(G_{S},U_{S}):=\rho_{*}\left(\mathcal{PW}(G_{S},U_{S})\right)
.
Proposition
9.1.1
gives that, as a linear functional on
\mathcal{PW}(G_{S},U_{S})
, the Plancherel measure
\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}
is of the form
\rho^{*}\mu
for some linear functional
\mu
on
\mathcal{PW}^{\mathrm{st}}(G_{S},U_{S})
.

The result then follows since
\bar{\rho}_{*}\circ\rho^{*}=\mathrm{id}
on the linear dual of
\mathcal{PW}^{\mathrm{st}}(G_{S},U_{S})
.
∎

We note a corollary that might be of independent interest (though it follows from the formal degree conjecture whenever that is known—e.g. from
[
BP21
]
):

Corollary 9.1.3
.

Let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in either the self-dual or conjugate self-dual case and
v
a finite place. For any supercuspidal
L
-packet
\Pi_{v}
of representations of
G_{v}
(i.e. whose
L
-parameter is irreducible and trivial on the Deligne-
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
), all
\pi_{v}\in\Pi_{v}
have the same formal degree.

Proof.

All representations
\pi_{v}
in a supercuspidal
L
-packet are supercuspidal
4
4

4

This is part of the construction of the LLC through the endoscopic classification.
. Therefore, their compactly supported matrix coefficients
c_{\pi_{v}}
satisfy that
\widehat{c}_{\pi_{v}}=\mathbf{1}_{\{\pi_{v}\}}
so that
c_{\pi_{v}}(1)
is the formal degree of
\pi_{v}
. The result then follows from applying Theorem
9.1.2
.
∎

9.2.
Positivity Test

We now derive from Theorem
9.1.2
a positivity test:

9.2.1.
Understanding
G_{S}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}

We first need to understand
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}
and
\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}
better through Harish-Chandra’s Plancherel formula. Without loss of generality, we assume
S=\{v\}
is a singleton. See
[
Sil96
]
for a review of the Plancherel formula which we will loosely follow and the proof of 3.5.1 in
[
Art13
]
for a very terse summary focused on applications to the endoscopic classification.

First,
\check{G}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{v}
decomposes into pieces called
\mathfrak{l}
-components. Consider the set of pairs
(M,\sigma)
of Levis
M
of
G_{v}
and discrete series (modulo the center)
\sigma
of
M
up to conjugation by
G_{v}
. If we fix a minimal Levi
M_{0}
and
M\supset M_{0}
, then the set
M^{*}/\Omega^{G}_{M}
of unramified unitary characters of
M
mod
\Omega^{G}_{M}:=N_{G_{v}}(M)
acts on the set of
(M^{\prime},\sigma)
with
M^{\prime}
conjugate to
M
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be the set of orbits of this action. Each
[M,\sigma]\in\mathcal{O}

can be given the structure of quotient of a product of circles by a finite group through the corresponding structure on
M^{*}/\Omega^{G}_{M}
. If
[M,\sigma]\in\mathcal{O}
, define

\check{G}_{v}[M,\sigma]:=\bigsqcup_{(M,\sigma)\in[M,\sigma]}\{\text{irred. subquotients }\pi\text{ of }\mathcal{I}_{P_{M}}^{G}(\sigma)\},

where
\mathcal{I}_{P_{M}}^{G}
is the normalized parabolic induction through a parabolic
P_{M}
with Levi factor
M
(its irreducible subquotients do not depend on the choice of
P
).

Then

\check{G}^{\mathrm{temp}}_{v}=\bigsqcup_{[M,\sigma]\in\mathcal{O}}\check{G}_{v}[M,\sigma].

In addition, for any open
U\subseteq[M,\sigma]
, the set

\bigsqcup_{(M,\sigma)\in U}\{\text{irred. subq. }\pi\text{ of }\mathcal{I}_{P_{M}}^{G}(\sigma)\}

is open in the Fell topology (this can be seen for example by the characterization of the restriction of the Fell topology to the infinite-dimensional irreducible representations in
\check{G}_{v}
in terms of matrix coefficients and the trace Paley-Weiner theorem).

We now establish an analoguous decomposition for
\check{G}_{v}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}
. If
\varphi
is a tempered parameter of
G
, there exists a Levi
M
such that
\varphi
is the unique-up-to-conjugation pushforward of discrete parameter
\varphi_{M}
for
M
. As part of the construction of LLC through the endoscopic classification,

(9.2.1)

\Pi_{\varphi}=\bigsqcup_{\sigma_{M}\in\Pi_{\varphi_{M}}}\{\text{irred. subq. }\pi\text{ of }\mathcal{I}_{P_{M}}^{G}(\sigma_{M})\}

(see the discussion before Proposition 6.6.1 in
[
Art13
]
and the discussion in §7.6 in
[
Mok15
]
).

Therefore, given a Levi
M
and discrete packet
\Pi_{M}
of
M
, we can similarly define a stable
\mathfrak{l}
-component
\check{G}^{\mathrm{st}}_{v}[M,\Pi_{M}]
which also inherits a real manifold-quotient structure from
M^{*}/\Omega^{G}_{M}
and gives disjoint union over an analogous
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{st}}
:

\check{G}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}_{v}=\bigsqcup_{[M,\Pi_{M}]\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{st}}}\check{G}^{\mathrm{st}}_{v}[M,\Pi_{M}].

In this way the map
\rho:\check{G}_{v}^{\mathrm{temp}}\to\check{G}_{v}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}
is continuous. Note also that since
\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}
is supported on all of
\check{G}_{v}^{\mathrm{temp}}
, its pushforward
\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}
is supported on all of
\check{G}_{v}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}
.

Our practical punchline for this discussion is that:

Lemma 9.2.1
.

Let
f_{v}
be a test function on
G_{v}
. Then
\rho_{*}\widehat{f}_{v}
is continuous on
\check{G}_{v}^{\mathrm{st},\mathrm{temp}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show this on each stable
\mathfrak{l}
-component
\check{G}^{\mathrm{st}}_{v}[M,\Pi_{M}]
. For each
\pi_{M}\in\Pi_{M}
,

\chi\mapsto\tr_{\mathcal{I}_{P_{M}}^{G}(\pi_{M}\otimes\chi)}f_{v}

is continuous on
\check{G}_{v}[M,\pi_{M}]
by the trace Paley-Wiener theorem
[
BDK86
]
(which actually gives much stronger regularity). However, by (
9.2.1
), the function
\rho_{*}\widehat{f}
on
\check{G}_{v}^{\mathrm{st}}[M,\Pi_{M}]
is just the sum of these functions over
\pi_{M}
.
∎

9.2.2.
Spectral Interpretation of Main Term

As a second input for the positivity test, we import some formalism from
[
Dal22
, §8.3]
to interpret (
6.2.1
) spectrally. For any group
H
, there is a central character map
\Omega:\check{H}\to\check{Z}_{H}
. Given a test function
f
on
H
and a central character
\omega
, we can define the conditional Plancherel expectation as the Radon-Nikodym derivative

E^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}|\omega):=\left.\frac{d\Omega_{*}(\widehat{f}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{H})}{d\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{Z_{H}}}\right|_{\omega}

which exists and is necessarily continuous as a function on
\check{Z}_{H}
.

Now, consider
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in either the self-dual or conjugate self-dual case. Let
f=\prod_{v\nmid\infty}f_{v}
be a test function on
G^{\infty}
such that
f_{v}=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{v}}
, for
K_{v}
a hyperspecial maximal compact, outside a finite set of places
S
. Then, as a special case of
[
Dal22
, Prop. 8.3.5]
, we can write

(9.2.2)

\sum_{\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)}\omega^{-1}(\gamma)f(\gamma)=C\sum_{\omega_{S}\in\Omega_{\lambda}(K^{S,\infty})}E^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}|\omega_{S})

for some constant
C>0
depending on various choices of measures and where

\Omega_{\lambda}(K^{S,\infty})=\{\omega_{S}\in\check{Z}_{G_{S}}:\omega_{S}|_{Z_{G}(F)\cap K^{S,\infty}}=\omega_{\lambda}|_{Z_{G}(F)\cap K^{S,\infty}}\}.

In many cases,
Z_{G_{S}}
is compact so
\check{Z}_{G_{S}}
is discrete. Then,
E^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}|\omega_{S})
simplifies as just the integral of
\widehat{f}\,d\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{G_{S}}
over some union of connected components of
\check{G}_{S}
.

9.2.3.
The Positivity Test

Putting everything together lets us show:

Proposition 9.2.2
.

Let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in either the self-dual or conjugate self-dual case.
Let
f
be a test function on
G(\mathbb{A})
equal to
\mathbf{1}_{K_{v}}
outside of a finite set of places
S
. Assume that for all tempered
L
-packets (or in the case of
\mathrm{SO}_{2n}
the appropriate unions of such)
\Pi_{S}
of
G_{S}
,

\tr_{\Pi_{S}}(f)\geq 0.

Then for any character
\omega
of
Z_{G,\infty}

\sum_{\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)}\omega^{-1}(\gamma)f(\gamma)\geq 0.

Furthermore, the inequality is strict if there exists a tempered
L
-packet
\Pi_{S}
such that
\tr_{\Pi_{S}}(f)>0
and
\omega_{\pi_{S}}|_{Z_{G}(F)\cap K^{S,\infty}}=\omega|_{Z_{G}(F)\cap K^{S,\infty}}
for all
\pi_{S}\in\Pi_{S}
.

Proof.

Consider any measurable
X\subseteq\check{Z}_{G_{S}}
and
G_{S,X}:=\Omega^{-1}(X)
. Then
\check{G}_{S,X}
is the exact preimage of a subset
\check{G}_{S,X}^{\mathrm{st}}
of
\check{G}_{S}^{\mathrm{st}}
since central characters are constant on
L
-packets by Lemma
5.2.2
. Then by the definition of
E^{\mathrm{pl}}
and Theorem
9.1.2
,

\int_{X}E^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}|\omega)\,d\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{Z_{G_{S}}}(\omega):=\int_{\check{G}_{S,X}}\tr_{\pi_{S}}(f_{S})\,d\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{G_{S}}(\pi_{S})\\
=\int_{\check{G}_{S,X}^{\mathrm{st}}}\sum_{\pi_{S}\in\Pi_{S}}\tr_{\pi_{S}}(f_{S})\,d\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{G_{S}}(\Pi_{S})=\int_{\check{G}_{S,\omega_{\lambda}}^{\mathrm{st}}}\tr_{\Pi_{S}}(f_{S})\,d\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{G_{S}}(\Pi_{S})\geq 0.

This forces
E^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}|\omega)\geq 0
for all
\omega
which, by (
9.2.2
), proves the non-negativity statement.

For the strict positivity result, if such a packet
\Pi_{S}
with appropriate central character
\omega_{0}
exists, then from Lemma
9.2.1
and the fact that every tempered packet is in the support of
\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{G_{S}}
, we get that
\rho_{*}(\widehat{f}_{S})\,d\bar{\rho}_{*}\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{G_{S}}
is supported at
\Pi_{S}
. By an argument similar to the previous paragraph, this implies that
E^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}|\omega)\,d\mu^{\mathrm{pl}}_{Z_{G_{S}}}
is supported at
\omega=\omega_{0}
. In particular, one summand
E^{\mathrm{pl}}(\widehat{f}_{S}|\omega_{0})
appearing in (
9.2.2
) is strictly positive.
∎

9.3.
Existence of Representations

Our strict positivity test requires the existence of local (conjugate) self-dual representations of
GL_{N}
with arbitrary conductor and specified central character. We use the shorthand notation for conductors, see §
3.1
.

9.3.1.
Self-dual case.

First note that the result fails for
N=1
. Self-dual characters of
F_{v}^{\times}
must be valued in
\pm 1
, and so have conductor at most
1
(though there always does exist a self-dual character of conductor 1.)

Lemma 9.3.1
.

Fix a place
v
, an integer
k\geq 0
, and a character
\omega:F_{v}^{\times}\to\pm 1
with conductor
\leq k
. Then there exists a self-dual tempered representation
\pi_{v}
of
GL_{2}(F_{v})
of conductor
c(\pi_{v})=k
and central character
\eta
. Moreover,

•

If
\eta
is trivial then
\pi_{v}
can be chosen to be symplectic for all
k
, or orthogonal for
k
even.

•

If
\eta
is nontrivial, then
\pi_{v}
is necessarily of orthogonal type. If it is cuspidal, then its parameter factors through
{{}^{L}}SO_{2}^{\eta}
.

Proof.

Self-dual representations of
GL_{2}
are characterized by the property that their central character is valued in
\pm 1
.

For
k
even and
\eta=1
, we can take
\pi_{v}
to be the principal series associated to characters
\chi\boxtimes\chi^{-1}
with
\chi
unitary of conductor
k/2
. This is self-dual with trivial central character and conductor
k
. The images of the non-irreducible parameters associated to these representations preserve both a symmetric and skew-symmetric form and can arise as the localizations of automorphic representations of either orthogonal or symplectic-type.

For
k\leq 2
and orthogonal type, we can consider the principal series induced from a pair of self-dual quadratic characters
\chi_{1}
,
\chi_{2}
such that
\chi_{1}\chi_{2}=\eta
and
c(\chi_{1})+c(\chi_{2})=k
.

For
k=1
and symplectic-type, consider the principal series induced from a pair of self-dual quadratic characters
\chi_{1}
,
\chi_{2}
such that
\chi_{1}\chi_{2}=\eta
and
c(\chi_{1})+c(\chi_{2})=1
(in the orthogonal case).

Finally, for the remaining cases with
k\geq 2
we rely on the construction of supercuspidals in
[
BH06
]
, from characters
\chi
of
L_{w}^{\times}
, for a quadratic extension
L_{w}/F_{v}
with ramification index
e
. In this construction, if
\chi
has conductor
\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{\ell}
, the resulting representation
\pi_{\chi}
has central character
\chi\mid_{F^{\times}_{v}}
and conductor
\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}
for
k=\frac{2\ell}{e}+2
, see
[
BH06
, Theorems 20.2 and 25.2]
. This reduces the existence question to that of characters of suitably ramified
L^{\times}_{w}
with a given restriction to
F_{v}^{\times}
and conductor, which follows from standard results on characters of finite abelian groups.

Supercuspidal representations correspond to irreducible
L
-parameters, which factor through a non-abelian subgroup of
{{}^{L}}G_{N}
. This subgroup is
{}^{L}\!{\mathrm{Sp}}_{2}={}^{L}\!{S}L_{2}
precisely when the central character
\eta=\chi\mid_{F^{\times}}
is trivial. Otherwise, the
L
-parameter is orthogonal and factors though
{}^{L}\!{S}O_{2}^{\eta}
.
∎

Lemma 9.3.2
.

Let
N>2
and let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
. Fix a place
v
and a conductor
k
, and, if
\widehat{G}
is orthogonal, a central character
\omega_{v}=\pm 1
, that can only be
-1
if
k>0
. Assume further that if
G_{v}=\mathrm{SO}_{N}^{\eta_{v}}
, then
k\geq c(\eta_{v})
.

Then there is a self-dual tempered representation
\pi_{v}
of
G_{N,v}
with:

•

conductor
k
,

•

L
-parameter factoring through
{}^{L}\!{G}_{v}
,

•

central character
\omega_{v}
on
G_{v}
.

Proof.

Let
m\in\{1,2\}
have the same parity as
N
and consider the representation

\pi_{v}=\chi_{1}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus\chi_{\lfloor\frac{N-1}{2}\rfloor}\boxplus\sigma_{m}\boxplus\chi^{-1}_{\lfloor\frac{N-1}{2}\rfloor}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus\chi^{-1}_{1},

for (not necessarily self-dual) characters
\chi_{i}
, and
\sigma_{m}
a self-dual unitary representation of
GL_{m}
. Then
\pi_{v}
is unitary with the same central character as
\sigma_{m}
and

c(\pi_{v})=c(\sigma_{m})+2\sum_{i}c(\chi_{i}),\qquad\epsilon(1/2,\pi_{v})=\epsilon(1/2,\sigma_{m})\prod_{i}\chi_{i}(-1).

In addition, by Lemma
9.3.1
, we can choose
\sigma_{m}
and the
\chi_{i}
so that
\pi_{v}
is tempered, has arbitrary conductor, and arbitrary self-dual central character
\eta_{v}
.

If
N
is odd, this
\pi_{v}
is necessarily orthogonal type and factors through
{}^{L}\!{G}={}^{L}\!{S}p_{N-1}^{\eta}
if the central character of
\pi_{v}
on
G_{N,v}
is
\eta_{v}
. In addition, if
c>0
and
N>2
, we can also choose the
\chi_{i}(-1)
and therefore the root number.
Since the root number determines the central character
\omega_{v}
on
G_{v}
by Theorem
5.2.1
,
\pi_{v}
can be chosen to satisfy all our desired conditions.

If
N
is even and
G=\mathrm{SO}_{N+1}
, we can choose
\sigma_{m}
to symplectic as in Lemma
9.3.1
, implying that
\pi_{v}
is too (as in the discussion on
[
Art13
, p. 34]
). Following Lemma
9.3.1
, the representation
\sigma_{m}
can be chosen so that
\pi_{v}
satisfies all our desired conditions.

Finally, for
G=\mathrm{SO}_{N}^{\eta}
, we choose
\sigma_{m}
to have parameter factoring through
{}^{L}\!{\mathrm{SO}}_{2}^{\eta}
as in Lemma
9.3.1
. Then, the parameter of
\pi_{v}
factors through
{}^{L}\!{\mathrm{SO}}_{N}^{\eta}
. We can also choose the
\chi_{i}(-1)
appropriately to determine the root number of
\pi_{v}
and therefore its central character.
∎

9.3.2.
Conjugate self-dual case

In the following lemmas, we fix a non-split place
v
of
F
and a place
w
of
E
above
v
. We use local indexing conventions for conductors (where ramified conductors are integers instead of half-integers, see
3.1.2
). Let

(9.3.1)

j_{v}:=\min\{1/2,\{j:1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{j}\subseteq N_{E/F}(E^{\times})\}\}.

The
1/2
is for indexing in order that the term
2j_{v}-1
, which will appear a lot in this section, satisfies

2j_{v}-1=\begin{cases}0&v\text{ unramified}\\
1&v\text{ tame}\\
\geq 1&v\text{ wild.}\end{cases}

Lemma 9.3.3
.

Let
\chi:E_{w}\to\mathbb{C}
be a conjugate self-dual character, so that restriction
\chi\mid_{F_{v}^{\times}}=\kappa\in\pm 1
is either trivial or the quadratic character corresponding to the extension
E_{w}
. Then the
L
-parameter of
\chi
factors through the endoscopic datum
U^{\kappa}_{1}
.

Proof.

We write down the explicit embeddings, following
[
Mok15
, §2.1]
. Using class field theory, we view the character
\chi
as a parameter

\varphi_{\chi}:\Gamma_{E_{w}}\to GL_{1}\times\Gamma_{E_{w}}.

Recall that

{}^{L}\!{G}(1)=(GL_{1}\times GL_{1})\rtimes\Gamma_{F_{v}}

where the action of
\Gamma_{F_{v}}
is through the quotient
\Gamma_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
and the nontrivial element swaps the two factors. Self-duality enables us to extend
\varphi_{\chi}
to a parameter

\varphi_{\chi}:\Gamma_{F_{v}}\to{}^{L}\!{G}(1)

as follows. Fixing a representative
z_{c}
of
\Gamma_{F_{v}}\setminus\Gamma_{E_{w}}
, we let:

\displaystyle\varphi_{\chi}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=(\chi(\sigma),\chi^{-1}(\sigma))\rtimes\sigma\quad\sigma\in\Gamma_{E_{w}}

\displaystyle\varphi_{\chi}(z_{c})

\displaystyle=(\alpha\chi(z_{c}^{2}),\alpha^{-1})\rtimes z_{c}.

In this way the value of
\varphi_{\chi}(z_{c})
is determined up to choice of a scalar
\alpha
(or, equivalently, up to a choice of representative
z_{c}
). Note that since
z_{c}^{2}
is invariant under conjugation by
z_{c}
, we must have
\chi(z_{c}^{2})=\chi(z_{c}^{2})^{-1}
, i.e.
\chi(z_{c}^{2})\in\{\pm 1\}
. Additionally, the condition that
z_{c}\notin\Gamma_{E_{w}}
implies by local class field theory that
\chi(z_{c}^{2})=\kappa
.

Next, recall that

{}^{L}\!{U}(1)=GL_{1}\rtimes\Gamma_{F_{v}}

where again, the action is via the quotient
\Gamma_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
and conjugation by a nontrivial element sends
g\mapsto g^{-1}.
Each embedding of
{}^{L}\!{U}(1)
into
{}^{L}\!{G}(1)
involves a choice: for
\kappa\in{\pm 1}
we fix an auxiliary character
\eta
of
E_{w}
whose restriction of
F_{v}^{\times}
is
\kappa
and we have

\displaystyle\xi_{\kappa}:{}^{L}\!{U}(1)

\displaystyle\to{}^{L}\!{G}(1)

\displaystyle\xi_{\kappa}(g)

\displaystyle=(g,g^{-1})\rtimes 1,\quad g\in GL_{1}(\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle\xi_{\kappa}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=(\eta(\sigma),\eta(\sigma^{-1}))\rtimes\sigma,\quad\sigma\in\Gamma_{E_{w}}

\displaystyle\xi_{\kappa}(z_{c})

\displaystyle=(\kappa,1)\rtimes z_{c}.

For the parameter
\varphi_{\chi}
to factor through
\xi_{\kappa}
, the choice of image of
z_{c}
must be compatible with the embedding, i.e. we must have
\kappa=\chi(z_{c}^{2})
, which gives the result.
∎

Lemma 9.3.4
.

Fix a conductor
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Then, for each
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(1)
, there exists a conjugate self-dual character of
E_{w}^{\times}
of conductor
k
such that the associated
L
-parameter factors through
{}^{L}\!{G}
if and only if one the following hold:

•

when
\kappa=1
:

–

E_{w}
is unramified,

–

E_{w}
is ramified and
k
is even,

•

when
\kappa=-1
:

–

E_{w}
is unramified

–

E_{w}
is ramified
and either:

*

k=2j_{v}-1
,

*

k\geq 2j_{v}
is even,

where
j_{v}
is as in (
9.3.1
).

Proof.

The condition of self-duality for characters is
\chi(\overline{g})=\chi(g^{-1})
, or equivalently, that
\chi|_{F_{v}^{\times}}\in\pm 1
, where we abuse notation and denote by
-1
the unique nontrival character of
F^{\times}/N(E^{\times}_{w})
where
N=N_{E_{w}/F_{v}}
is the norm map. The sign
\pm 1
determines which element of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{sim}}(1)
the parameter
\chi
factors through, as in Lemma
9.3.3
.

Next, denote
U^{0}=\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}^{\times}
and
U^{k}=1+\mathfrak{p}_{w}^{k}
. For indexing convenience, denote
U^{-1}
to be some set strictly bigger than
E^{\times}_{w}
. We now consider necessary and sufficient conditions for the existence of a character of sign
\kappa
and conductor
k
:

\kappa=1

There exists a character of sign
\kappa=1
and conductor
k
if and only if

(9.3.2)

U^{k-1}\not\subset F^{\times}U^{k}.

For
k=0
, this condition is empty. For
k>0
, we have

\text{\eqref{eq obs kappa = 1}}\iff U^{k-1}\not\subset(F^{\times}\cap U^{k-1})U^{k}\iff(U^{k-1}\cap F)/(U^{k}\cap F)\not\simeq U^{k-1}/U^{k}

and, by comparing cardinalities, the latter holds if any only if

•

E_{w}
is unramified, or

•

E_{w}
is ramified and
k
is even.

\kappa=-1
:

To determine a necessary and sufficient condition for existence here, we use that given two characters
\chi_{1}
,
\chi_{2}
with conductors
c(\chi_{1})>c(\chi_{2})
, then
c(\chi_{1}\chi_{2})=c(\chi_{1})
. Furthermore, the parities satisfy
\kappa(\chi_{1}\chi_{2})=\kappa(\chi_{1})\kappa(\chi_{2})
. In particular, if
k_{0}
is the smallest possible conductor for which there exists
\chi
with
\kappa=-1
, then for all
k>k_{0}
, conductor
k
is possible with
\kappa=-1
if and only if it is with
\kappa=1
. Therefore, it suffices to find this
k_{0}
.

If
E_{w}
is unramified, then any character
\chi
with
\chi(\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times})\equiv 1
and such that
\chi
takes some uniformizer to
-1
is unramified and has
\kappa=-1
. Therefore,
k_{0}=0
.

If
E_{w}
is ramified, note the existence of a character
\chi
with
\kappa=-1
requires that

F^{\times}\not\subset N(E^{\times})U^{k}\iff F^{\times}\cap U^{k}\subseteq N(E^{\times}).

This forces
k\geq 2j-1
. To construct
\chi
with
k=2j-1
, it suffices to further note that

1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{j-1}\notin N(E^{\times})\implies U^{2j-2}\notin N(E^{\times})U^{2j-1}

Therefore,
k_{0}=2j-1
.
∎

The full list of possible pairs
(k,l)
of conductor
c(\pi_{v})=k
and central character conductor
c(\omega_{\pi_{v}})=l
is very hard to determine and likely does not have a nice description, particularly in the wildly ramified case. We settle for providing an easy-to-describe subset that covers most relevant possibilities outside of some wild ramification issues.

Lemma 9.3.5
.

Let
N\geq 4
be even, and let
G=U_{N}^{+}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in the conjugate self-dual case. Fix a non-split place
v
and let
e_{v}:=e(E_{w}:F_{v})
be the ramification index of
E_{w}/F_{v}
. Fix
k\geq 0
a conductor, and fix a central character
\omega_{v}
on
G_{v}
transferring to
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
on
G_{N,v}
with conductor
l\leq k
.

Then there is a conjugate self-dual tempered representation
\pi_{v}
of
G_{N,v}
with conductor
c(\pi_{v})=k
such that its corresponding
L
-parameter
\psi_{\pi_{v}}
factors through
{}^{L}\!{G}_{v}
and has central character
\omega_{v}
on
G_{v}
for the following cases:

•

l=\max\{k-(2j_{v}-1),0\}
when
e_{v}=1
and the largest even number less than this otherwise and:

–

v
unramified: all possible
k
,

–

v
tamely ramified: all possible
k
,

–

v
wildly ramified:
k\leq N/2
or
k>4j_{v}-2
.

•

l=0
:

–

v
unramified: all possible
k
,

–

v
tamely ramified: all possible
k
,

–

v
wildly ramified:
k\leq N/2
or
k\geq 8j_{v}-4

Proof.

First, note that
k=l=0
is always achievable by looking at a self-dual Satake parameter.

Otherwise, consider
\pi_{v}
to be the Langlands quotient of the parabolic induction of blocks

\rho_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\rho_{t}

where each
\rho_{i}
is a conjugate-symplectic representation of some even
G_{N_{i},v}
. It suffices to consider the case where all
\rho_{i}
are trivial except for one that is a principal series with central character
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
and possibly some number that are dimension-2 Steinbergs.

Since
c(\omega_{\pi_{v}})=\sum_{i}c(\omega_{\rho_{i}})
and
c(\pi_{v})=\sum_{i}c(\rho_{i})
, it suffices to describe the possible values of conductor
c(\rho_{i})=k
and central character conductor
c(\omega_{\rho_{i}})=l
that can be achieved with our list of possible
\rho_{i}
and choose appropriately amongst these options.

Rank
2
Principal Series:

Let
\chi_{0}
be a choice of conjugate-symplectic character of
E_{v}^{\times}
with minimum conductor as in the proof of Lemma
9.3.4
. Choose conjugate orthogonal
\nu_{1}\nu_{2}=\omega^{\prime}_{v}
with conductors
k_{1},k_{2}
respectively. Consider
\rho_{i}
the parabolic induction of
\chi_{0}\nu_{1}\boxtimes\chi_{0}^{-1}\nu_{2}
which is conjugate-symplectic since the components are (see the discussion after Definition 2.4.7 in
[
Mok15
]
).

There are two possible cases for conductors:

•

k_{1}=k_{2}\geq l
: Then
k=\max\{2k_{1},4j_{v}-2\}
.

•

k_{1}=l
and
k_{1}>k_{2}
: Then
k=\max\{l,2j-1\}+\max\{k_{2},2j_{v}-1\}
.

Note in the above computations that in the ramified cases when
j_{v}\neq 0
, the
k_{i}
are always even and therefore not equal to
2j_{v}-1
. Therefore, we never need to worry about the case where the conductor of a product with
\chi_{0}
is not the maximum of its factors.

This in particular realizes the following possible pairs
(k,l)
(with
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
arbitrary of conductor
l
):

•

v
unramified:
k
even and
l=0
or
k
arbitrary and
k=l

•

v
tamely ramified:
k\geq 4
a multiple of
4
and
l=0
,
k=2
and
l=0
, or
k>2
odd and
l=k-1
.

•

v
wildly ramified:
k\geq 4j_{v}
a multiple of
4
and
l=0
,
k=4j_{v}-2
and
l=0
, or
k>4j_{v}-2
odd and
l=k-(2j_{v}-1)
.

Rank-
4
Principal Series:

In the ramified cases when
l=0
, we also need to consider dimension-
4
principal series. Choose
\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\nu_{3}=1
and
\rho_{i}
the parabolic induction of

\chi_{0}\nu_{1}\boxtimes\chi_{0}\nu_{2}\boxtimes\chi_{0}^{-1}\nu_{3}\boxtimes\chi_{0}^{-1}.

We consider the case when
c(\nu_{0})=c(\nu_{1})=k_{2}\geq c(\nu_{2})=k_{2}
. Then
\rho_{i}
has conductor

k=\max\{2k_{1},4j_{v}-2\}+\max\{k_{2},2j_{v}-1\}+(2j_{v}-1)

which can be any integer larger than
8j_{v}-4
.

Steinberg

If
\rho_{i}
is the Steinberg, then it gives
k=1
and
l=0
.

Even-Rank Trivial

If
\rho_{i}
is trivial with even rank, then it is both conjugate-orthogonal and conjugate-symplectic. This gives
k=0
and
l=0
.
∎

Note
.

Constructing representations of odd conductor when
N
is even crucially depended on choosing
G=U^{+}_{N}
through the possible choice of characters being induced. This is a good consistency check for Proposition
8.3.1
: when
G=U^{-}_{N}
, the function
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{v,k}=\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{v,k}
transfers to
0
when
k
is odd. Given Proposition
9.2.2
, this is incompatible with the existence of odd-conductor representations and sign
\kappa=-1
.

At split places things are simpler:

Lemma 9.3.6
.

Let
N\geq 3
, and let
G=U_{N}^{\pm}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
in the conjugate self-dual case. Fix a
split
place
v
and a conductor
k
. Fix a central character
\omega_{v}
on
G_{v}
transferring to
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
on
G_{N,v}
with conductor
l\leq k
.

Then there is a conjugate self-dual tempered representation
\pi_{v}
of
G_{N,v}
with conductor
c(\pi_{v})=k
such that its corresponding
L
-parameter
\psi_{\pi_{v}}
factors through
{}^{L}\!{G}_{v}
and has central character
\omega_{v}
on
G_{v}
.

Proof.

Any such
\pi_{v}
on
G_{N,v}\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{v})\times\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{v})
is of the form
\pi_{0}\boxtimes(\pi_{0})^{\vee}
. In the split place, the
\kappa=\pm
embeddings are the same, so this has parameter factoring through both. In our conventions, the conductor of
\pi_{v}
is that of
\pi_{0}
, so it suffices to find
\pi_{0}
of conductor
k
and central character
\omega^{\prime}_{v}=\omega_{v}\circ\phi
. This can be done using principal series.
∎

9.4.
Application to
(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}

Now we are finally ready to show when our main term (
6.2.1
) for
f^{\infty}=(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}
is positive.

9.4.1.
Self-Dual Case

Since it doesn’t take that much extra work in the self-dual case, we prove a statement for all
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
instead of just
G=\mathrm{SO}_{N+1}
when
N
is even (though note that for this eventual case of interest,
Z_{G}
is trivial so the statement can be made simpler):

Theorem 9.4.1
.

Let
\widetilde{G}_{N}
in the self-dual case and
\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
the test function on
\widetilde{G}_{N}^{\infty}
defined in
4.4.5
. Choose
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
and let
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
be the corresponding central characters on
Z_{G_{N},v}
common to all transfers from
G_{v}
(as in the discussion after Lemma
5.2.3
).

Fix a central character
\omega_{\infty}
on
Z_{G_{\infty}}\subseteq\{\pm 1\}
and assume:

•

For all
v
,
v(\mathfrak{n})\geq c(\omega^{\prime}_{v})
,

•

If
\omega_{\infty}\neq 1
,
\mathfrak{n}\neq 1
.

Then,

\sum_{\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)}\omega_{\infty}^{-1}(\gamma)(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(\gamma)>0.

Proof.

We apply Proposition
9.2.2
to the set
S
containing all primes on which
\mathfrak{n}
is supported and all finite places at which
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
is nontrivial. The input for non-negativity follows from the defining property Corollary
4.4.4
of
\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
plugged into the endoscopic character identities
[
Art13
, Thm 2.2.1(a)]
or
[
Mok15
, Thm 3.2.1(a)]
.

For strict positivity, if
\omega_{\infty}\neq 1
, choose
v
such that
v(\mathfrak{n})>0
and set
\omega_{v}
to be the non-trivial character on
Z_{G_{v}}
. Set all other
\omega_{v^{\prime}}=1
. Then, Lemma
9.3.2
constructs representations
\pi_{v}
on each
G_{N,v}
of conductor
\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{v(\mathfrak{n})}
, central character
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
, and that corresponds to a packet on
G_{v}
with central character
\omega_{v}
.

Then, if
\pi_{S}=\prod_{v\in S}\pi_{v}
as a representation of
G_{N,S}
, it has conductor
\mathfrak{n}
and its corresponding
A
-packet
\Pi_{S}
on
G_{S}
is made of
\pi_{S}
with central character satisfying
\omega_{\pi_{S}}|_{Z_{G}(F)}=\omega_{\pi_{\infty}}|_{Z_{G}(F)}
. Therefore,
\Pi_{S}
is the necessary input to Proposition
9.2.2
for strict positivity (again by the endoscopic character identities).
∎

9.4.2.
Conjugate Self-dual case

In the conjugate self-dual case, globalizing central characters is more complicated. For
v
a place of
F
, let
e_{v}
be its ramification degree in
E
. For
\mathfrak{n}
a conductor, let
\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
be the units that are
1\pmod{\mathfrak{n}}
.

We return to global indexing for conductors (where ramified exponents are half-integers).

Lemma 9.4.2
.

Let
G=U^{+}_{N}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
. Then for each finite place
v
of
F
there is
b_{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-2}
depending only on
E/F
such that for all

•

\omega_{\infty}
a character on
Z_{G_{\infty}}
that transfers to
\omega^{\prime}_{\infty}
on
Z_{G_{N},\infty}
,

•

conductor
\mathfrak{n}
such that

(i)

\mathfrak{n}
is an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
(i.e.
v(\mathfrak{n})
is an integer for all ramified
v
),

(ii)

v(\mathfrak{n})\geq b_{v}+2/e_{v}
for one place or
v(\mathfrak{n})\geq b_{v}+1/e_{v}
for at least two places,

there exists a global
\omega^{\prime}
on
Z_{G_{N}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
with infinite component
\omega^{\prime}_{\infty}
and such that
{\omega^{\prime}}^{\infty}
has conductor exactly
\mathfrak{n}
.

Proof.

Let
\phi(x)=\frac{x}{\bar{x}}
be the map first defined in §
8
. Let
\Xi
be the roots of unity in
E
and for each finite place
v
of
F
, consider the smallest
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
\Xi\cap\phi(1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{x})=1
. Set
b_{v}=x
if
x>0
and
b_{v}=-2
if
x=0
. Note that we are not allowing half-integral
b_{v}
when
v
is ramified.

The
b_{v}
are chosen exactly so that if
\mathfrak{n}
is a conductor satisfying conditions (i)-(ii) and
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
is a proper divisor of
\mathfrak{n}
, then there is intermediate proper divisor
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Xi\cap\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}))=1
. The integrality condition guarantees that there is at least one conjugate-orthogonal character of conductor
\mathfrak{n}
by Lemma
9.3.4
, so in addition
\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}))\neq\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}))
.

Next,
Z_{G}(F)\cap Z_{G}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})=\Xi
since it consists of integral elements of
E^{\times}
that have complex norm one in all their embeddings. It follows that for any compact open
K^{\infty}\subseteq Z_{G}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})
,

Z_{G}(F)\backslash Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/K^{\infty}=Z_{G}(F^{\infty})/(\Xi\cap K^{\infty})\times Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{F})/Z_{G}(F)K^{\infty}.

If
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}
is a proper divisor of
\mathfrak{n}
such that
\Xi\cap\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}))=1
, and
\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}))\neq\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}))
, note that:

Z_{G}(F)\backslash Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}))\neq Z_{G}(F)\backslash Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}))\implies\\
Z_{G}(F_{\infty})\times Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{F})/Z_{G}(F)\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}))\neq Z_{G}(F_{\infty})\times Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{F})/Z_{G}(F)\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}))\\
\implies Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{F})/Z_{G}(F)\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}))\neq Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{F})/Z_{G}(F)\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime})).

Therefore, the properties (i)-(ii) of
\mathfrak{n}
guarantees that we can globalize
\omega_{\infty}
to a character
\omega
of
Z_{G}(F)\backslash Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}))
that doesn’t descend to
Z_{G}(F)\backslash Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})/\phi(\mathcal{O}_{E}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}))
for any proper divisor
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{n}
.

Setting
\omega^{\prime}=\omega\circ\phi
then suffices by Lemma
5.2.4
.
∎

Note
.

In many cases,
\Xi\cap(1+\mathfrak{D}_{E/F})=0
already, so the condition on
\mathfrak{n}
in Lemma
9.4.2
is trivial (i.e. all
b_{v}
can be chosen to be
-2
).

Theorem 9.4.3
.

Let
N\geq 4
be even,
G=U^{+}_{N}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
, and
\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
the test function on
\widetilde{G}^{\infty}_{N}
defined in
4.4.5
. Let
\omega_{\infty}
be a central character of
G_{\infty}
. Choose
b_{v}
as in Lemma
9.4.2
and let
\mathfrak{n}
be a conductor such that

•

For all wildly ramified
v
,
v(\mathfrak{n})\leq N/4
or
v(\mathfrak{n})>2j_{v}-1
,

•

There is at least one
v
such that
\max\{\lfloor v(\mathfrak{n})-(j_{v}-1/2)\rfloor,0\}\geq b_{v}+2/e_{v}
or there are at least two
v
such that
\max\{\lfloor v(\mathfrak{n})-(j_{v}-1/2)\rfloor,0\}\geq b_{v}+1/e_{v}
.

Then,

\sum_{\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)}\omega_{\infty}^{-1}(\gamma)(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(\gamma)>0.

Proof.

Define the modified conductor

\mathfrak{n}_{c}=\prod_{v}\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{\max\{\lfloor v(\mathfrak{n})-(j_{v}-1/2)\rfloor,0\}}.

By Lemma
9.4.2
, we can find a character
\omega^{\prime}
on
Z_{G_{N}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
such that
\omega^{\prime}_{\infty}
transfers to
\omega_{\infty}^{-1}
and
\omega^{\prime}
has conductor
\mathfrak{n}_{c}
. Then, our
assumptions on
\mathfrak{n}
let us proceed as in Theorem
9.4.1
: multiplying together the outputs of Lemmas
9.3.5
and
9.3.6
(using the
l\neq 0
option for inputs in Lemma
9.3.5
) gives a representation
\pi^{\infty}
of
G_{N}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
with central character
(\omega^{\prime})^{\infty}
and conductor
\mathfrak{n}
.

The argument then follows exactly as Theorem
9.4.1
.
∎

We also prove a version for trivial
\omega_{\infty}
:

Theorem 9.4.4
.

Let
N\geq 4
be even,
G=U^{+}_{N}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
, and
\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
the test function on
\widetilde{G}^{\infty}_{N}
defined in
4.4.5
. Choose the conductor
\mathfrak{n}
such that:

•

For all wildly ramified
v
,
v(\mathfrak{n})\leq N/4
or
v(\mathfrak{n})>4j_{v}-2
.

Then,

\sum_{\gamma\in Z_{G}(F)}(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(\gamma)>0.

Proof.

This is the same argument as Theorem
9.4.1
but instead of globalizing
\omega_{\infty}
using Lemma
9.4.2
, we globalize trivial
\omega_{\infty}
to the trivial character and use the
l=0
option for inputs in Lemma
9.3.5
.
∎

Note
.

A more careful analysis of representations in Lemma
9.3.5
could increase the generality of wildly ramified factors allowed in
\mathfrak{n}
. Furthermore, by inspection of the proof of Lemma
9.3.5
, there are similarly results for
N=2
, but with some extra congruence conditions on the exponents of ramified primes dividing
\mathfrak{n}
.

10.
Final Result

We now discuss implications for equidistribution of epsilon factors:

10.1.
General Setup

We first describe sets of automorphic representations.

Definition 10.1.1
.

In either the self-dual or conjugate self-dual case, let
N>0
, and consider
\mathfrak{n}
a conductor for
F
and
\lambda
a (conjugate) self-dual infinitesimal character for
G_{N,\infty}
. Choose
\star
to be either (conjugate) orthogonal or (conjugate) symplectic—denoted by “
\mathrm{orth}
” or “
\mathrm{symp}
” respectively.

Define
\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\star}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})
to be the set of self-dual, type-
\star
, cuspidal automorphic representations
\pi
of
\mathrm{GL}_{N}
of conductor
\mathfrak{n}
and with infinitesimal character
\lambda
at infinity.

The most basic question we can ask about distribution of root numbers
\epsilon(1/2,\pi)
in
\mathcal{AR}_{N,\star}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})
, is to understand asymptotics of

\frac{1}{|\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\star}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})|}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N,\star}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}\epsilon(1/2,\pi).

In full generality, we also want to understand weighted distributions—if
\widetilde{f}_{S}
is a test function at some set of places
S
coprime to
\mathfrak{n}
, we can try to compute the asymptotics of

\frac{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\star}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{S}}(\tilde{f}_{S})\epsilon(1/2,\pi)}{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\star}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{S}}(\widetilde{f}_{S})},

where we recall
\widetilde{\pi}
is the canonical extension of
\pi
to
\widetilde{G}_{N}
.

We first describe the class of functions
f_{S}
which our methods allow us to weigh the count by:

Definition 10.1.2
.

In either the self-dual or conjugate self-dual case, let
S
be a finite set of finite places of
F
and
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
. Then a test function
\tilde{f}_{S}\in\mathscr{H}(\widetilde{G}_{N,S})
is a full weighting for
G_{S}
if the Fourier transform of
(\tilde{f}_{S})^{G}|_{Z_{G_{S}}}
is strictly positive on all unramified characters.

Note that if
(\tilde{f}_{S})^{G}|_{Z_{G_{S}}}
is supported in
K_{S}\cap Z_{G_{S}}
(in particular, if
Z_{G_{S}}
is compact), this simplifies to the condition that
(\tilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)>0
.

Test functions that are not weightings produce sums over automorphic representations that cancel out except for an asymptotically trivial subset of
\mathcal{AR}_{\star}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})
, making them much harder to study.

Some examples of full weightings for
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
:

•

Indicators of hyperspecials
\widetilde{f}_{S}=\mathbf{1}_{K_{S}\rtimes\theta}
give unweighted counts.

•

For any test function
f_{S}\in\mathscr{H}(G_{S})
, we can find
\tilde{f}_{S}\in\mathscr{H}(\widetilde{G}_{N})
such that
(\tilde{f}_{S})^{G}=f_{S}
by
[
Mok15
, Prop 3.1.1(b)]
or
[
Art13
, Prop. 2.1.1]
(the argument for Mok’s extension part (b) also works in Arthur’s case).

•

If
\widetilde{f}_{S}
satisfies that
\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{S}}(f_{S})\geq 0
for all (conjugate) self-dual
\pi_{S}
and there are tempered
A
-parameters
\psi_{S}
for
G
with general enough central character such that
\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi_{S}}}(f_{S})>0
, then
f_{S}
will be a full weighting similarly to Proposition
9.2.2
.

10.2.
Self-Dual Case

In the self-dual case,
\epsilon(1/2,\pi)=1
unless
N
is even and
\pi
is symplectic-type/comes from
G=\mathrm{SO}_{N+1}
. We focus on this case:

Theorem 10.2.1
.

Let
N>0
be even, and consider
\mathfrak{n}
