a conductor for
F
and
\lambda
self-dual, symplectic-type, integral infinitesimal character for
G_{N,\infty}
as in §
5.1.2
.
Choose a finite set of finite places
S
coprime to
\mathfrak{n}
and a test function

\widetilde{f}_{S}:=\mathbf{1}_{K_{S}\rtimes\theta}\star f_{S}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\widetilde{G}_{N}(F_{S}))^{\leq\kappa},

such that
\widetilde{f}_{S}
is a full weighting for
G=\mathrm{SO}_{N+1}
. For any self-dual automorphic representation
\pi
of
\mathrm{GL}_{N}
, define coefficients

w(\pi):=\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{S}}(\widetilde{f}_{S}).

with respect to the canonical extension of
\pi
to
\widetilde{G}_{N}
. Then, if there is place
v
such that either

•

v(\mathfrak{n})>N
,

•

v(\mathfrak{n})
is odd,

we have

\frac{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)\epsilon(1/2,\pi)}{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)}=O_{\mathfrak{n}}(m(\lambda)^{-C}q_{S}^{A+B\kappa})

for some constants
A,B,C
with
C>1
. Otherwise,

\frac{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)\epsilon(1/2,\pi)}{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)}\\
=\epsilon(1/2,\varphi_{\lambda})(-1)^{\sum_{v}v(\mathfrak{n})/2}R+O_{\mathfrak{n}}(m(\lambda)^{-C}q_{S}^{A+B\kappa})

for some
R>0
and where
\varphi_{\lambda}
is the parameter with infinitesimal character
\lambda
and trivial central character (i.e. the one coming from the discrete
L
-packet with infinitesimal character
\lambda
on
G
).

Proof.

Let
\Sigma_{\eta}
be the shape containing only cuspidal parameters of symplectic-type—i.e.
H(\Sigma_{\eta})=\mathrm{SO}_{2N+1}=G
. By Theorem
4.3.5
and Proposition
6.1.1
,

\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)\epsilon(1/2,\pi)=\epsilon(1/2,\varphi_{\lambda})\sum_{\psi\in\Sigma_{\eta,\lambda}}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})\\
=\epsilon(1/2,\varphi_{\lambda})S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G}),

after noting that
S
coprime to
\mathfrak{n}
means that
\widetilde{E}_{\mathfrak{n},S}=\mathbf{1}_{K_{S}\rtimes\theta}
.

By similar arguments to
[
Dal22
, §5.4]
, we have
(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G)^{\leq D\kappa}
for some constant
D
depending only on
N
and
G
. Therefore, by the bound of Proposition
6.3.2
with our
S
:

(10.2.1)

(\dim\lambda)^{-1}S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})\\
=\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})+O_{\mathfrak{n}}(m(\lambda)^{-C}q_{S}^{A+B\kappa}(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)),

where
\Lambda
is from (
6.2.1
) and for some
A,B,C
with
C\geq 1
. By Corollary
7.2.2
, we can compute

\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})=(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(1)(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)\\
=(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)\begin{cases}0&\mathfrak{n}\text{ satisfies conditions}\\
M(-1)^{\sum_{v}v(\mathfrak{n})/2}&\text{else}\end{cases}

for some constant
M>0
.

By a similar argument, now using Corollary
4.4.4
,

\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)=S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})

and

(10.2.2)

(\dim\lambda)^{-1}S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})\\
=(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(1)(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)+O_{\mathfrak{n}}(m(\lambda)^{-C}q_{S}^{A+B\kappa}(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)).

By Theorem
9.4.1
,
(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}(1)>0
. The result follows from dividing (
10.2.1
) by (
10.2.2
).
∎

In particular, when
\mathfrak{n}
satisfies the bulleted conditions, the root numbers
\epsilon(1/2,\pi)
for
\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})
become equidistributed between
\pm 1
as
m(\lambda)\to\infty
. This equidistribution is “independent” of any full weighting by Weyl-invariant polynomials on the self-dual Satake parameters at places coprime to
\mathfrak{n}
. There are also power-saving bounds on how fast equidistribution might be achieved.

If
\mathfrak{n}
does not satisfy the bulleted conditions, then there is an “alternating” asymptotic bias between
\epsilon(1/2,\pi)=\pm 1
.

10.3.
Conjugate Self-Dual Case

In the conjugate self-dual case, our techniques only apply when
N
is even. Then,
\epsilon(1/2,\pi)=1
unless
\pi
is conjugate symplectic/comes from
G=U^{+}_{N}
. We focus on this case.

Isolating the conditions from Theorem
9.4.3
:

Definition 10.3.1
.

For a place
v
of
F
, let
e_{v}
be the ramification degree, and recall the numbers
b_{v}
from Lemma
9.4.2
and
j_{v}
from §
9.3.2
. Call a conductor
\mathfrak{n}
(as in §
3.1.2
) valid if

•

For all wildly ramified
v
,
v(\mathfrak{n})\leq N/4
or
v(\mathfrak{n})>2j_{v}-1
,

•

There is at least one
v
such that
\max\{\lfloor v(\mathfrak{n})-(j_{v}-1/2)\rfloor,0\}\geq b_{v}+2/e_{v}
or there are at least two
v
such that
\max\{\lfloor v(\mathfrak{n})-(j_{v}-1/2)\rfloor,0\}\geq b_{v}+1/e_{v}
.

Call it
0
-valid if

•

For all wildly ramified
v
,
v(\mathfrak{n})\leq N/4
or
v(\mathfrak{n})>4j_{v}-2
,

(see note
Note
for potentially weaker conditions.)

We repeat again that these conditions, while not comprehensive, are quite weak: for example, only the second part of the valid condition doesn’t involve wild ramification and it is automatically satisfied if there are no roots of unity in
1+\mathfrak{D}_{E/F}
.

Theorem 10.3.2
.

Let
N>2
be even and let
\lambda
be a conjugate self-dual infinitesimal character of
G_{N}
as in §
5.1.2
. Let
\mathfrak{n}
be a conductor for
F
. Assume that
\mathfrak{n}
is valid or, if
\lambda
corresponds to the trivial central character, possibly instead
0
-valid.

Choose a finite set of finite places
S
coprime to
\mathfrak{n}
and a test function

\widetilde{f}_{S}:=\mathbf{1}_{K_{S}\rtimes\theta}\star f_{S}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(\widetilde{G}_{N}(F_{S}))^{\leq\kappa},

such that
\widetilde{f}_{S}
is a full weighting for
G=U^{+}_{N}
. For any conjugate self-dual automorphic representation
\pi
of
G_{N}
, define coefficients

w(\pi):=\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{S}}(\widetilde{f}_{S})

with respect to the canonical extension of
\pi
to
\widetilde{G}_{N}
. Then, if there is a ramified place
v
such that either

•

v(\mathfrak{n})
is half-integral,

•

v(\mathfrak{n})>N/2
,

we have

\frac{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)\epsilon(1/2,\pi)}{\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)}=O_{\mathfrak{n},\omega_{\lambda}}(m(\lambda)^{-C}q_{S}^{A+B\kappa})

for some constants
A,B,C
with
C>1
and where
\omega_{\lambda}
is the central character of the finite-dimensional representation of
G_{\infty}
corresponding to
\lambda
.

Proof.

This is the same argument as Theorem
10.2.1
except we use the analogous results for the conjugate self-dual case.

Theorem
4.3.6
and Proposition
6.1.1
interpret

\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)\epsilon(1/2,\pi)=\omega_{\infty}(a)^{-1}\epsilon(1/2,\varphi_{\lambda})^{-1}S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})

for some choice of
a/\bar{a}=-1
. Then, by Theorem
6.4.1
:

(\dim\lambda)^{-1}S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})\\
=\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})+O_{\mathfrak{n}}(m(\lambda)^{-C}q_{S}^{A+B\kappa}(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)),

where our bulleted conditions on
\mathfrak{n}
give that

\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})=0

by Corollary
8.4.1
.

On the other hand, Corollary
4.4.6
gives that

\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{AR}_{N}^{\mathrm{symp}}(\lambda,\mathfrak{n})}w(\pi)=S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})

and Theorem
6.4.1
again gives

(\dim\lambda)^{-1}S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})\\
=\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})+O_{\mathfrak{n}}(m(\lambda)^{-C}q_{S}^{A+B\kappa}(\widetilde{f}_{S})^{G}(1)).

Let the support of
\mathfrak{n}
be
S_{0}
. Then by (
9.2.2
),

\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})=\sum_{\omega_{S_{0}}\omega_{S}\in\Omega_{\lambda}(K^{S,S_{0},\infty})}E^{\mathrm{pl}}((\widetilde{C}_{S_{0},\mathfrak{n}})^{G}f_{S}^{G}|\omega_{S_{0}}\omega_{S})

for the set of characters
\Omega_{\lambda}(K^{S,S_{0},\infty})
of
Z_{G,S\cup S_{0}}
defined there. Each of the summands factors into:

E^{\mathrm{pl}}((\widetilde{C}_{S_{0},\mathfrak{n}})^{G}|\omega_{S_{0}})E^{\mathrm{pl}}(f_{S}^{G}|\omega_{S}).

Since
f_{S}^{G}
is unramified, the second factor is zero unless
\omega_{S}|_{K_{S}}
is trivial. Therefore, we can rewrite
\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})
as

\sum_{\omega_{S_{0}}\in\Omega_{\lambda}(K^{S_{0},\infty})}E^{\mathrm{pl}}((\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}|\omega_{S_{0}})\left(\sum_{\omega_{S_{0}}\omega_{S}\in\Omega_{\lambda}(K^{S,S_{0},\infty})}E^{\mathrm{pl}}(f_{S}^{G}|\omega_{S})\right)

Since
f_{S}
is a full weighting, the values of the inner sum are always strictly positive. By Theorem
9.4.3
or
9.4.4
, trace-positivity of
\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
, and validity of
\mathfrak{n}
, all the terms
E^{\mathrm{pl}}((\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})^{G}|\omega_{S_{0}})
are non-negative and at least one of them is strictly positive. In total,
\Lambda(G,\omega_{\lambda},(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}\star f_{S})^{G})>0
.

Therefore, we get our result by dividing these two computations.
∎

Because we cannot prove Conjecture
8.4.3
, we do not have a non-equidistribution result analoguous to the second part of Theorem
10.2.1
. However, we expect that it does hold for valid (or
0
-valid)
\mathfrak{n}
that do not satisfy the bulleted conditions.

As a replacement, we could use the
\tilde{f}_{\mathfrak{n}}
from Corollary
8.4.1
in our trace formula computation instead of
\widetilde{E}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}
. This would prove a non-equidistribution result for an average over
\pi
with
c(\pi)|\mathfrak{n}
that is weighted by a product depending on
\mathfrak{n}/c(\pi)
of the (signed!) coefficients from Proposition
4.2.5
.
